\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\not\in\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{p}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{p}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})}\langle{P},{\sigma[{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}\mapsto{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}]}\rangle}\end{array}\hfill\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SSend]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}{e}\downarrow\sigma=\mathsf{v}}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\,{e}{}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}}\langle{},{}\rangle}\end{array}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[27.74982pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SRcv]}$}\\
{\langle{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}(x_{i}).P_{i}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}{\mathsf{v}}}\langle{P_{j}},{\sigma[{x_{j}}\mapsto{\mathsf{v}}]}\rangle\,j\in I}\end{array}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SSeq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{P;Q},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime};Q},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\\[27.74982pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SThen]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}{e}\downarrow\sigma=\mathtt{true}\hskip 9.24994pt\langle{P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{~{e}~~P~~{Q}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\land e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SElse]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}{e}\downarrow\sigma=\mathtt{false}\hskip 9.24994pt\langle{Q},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{Q^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{~{e}~~P~~{Q}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{\neg e\land e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{Q^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\\[27.74982pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SFor]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}{\ell}\downarrow\sigma\neq\varepsilon\ \hskip 18.49988pt\langle{P},{\sigma[{x}\mapsto{headLcolor]{hd}({\ell}\downarrow\sigma)}]}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{~{x}~~{\ell}~~{P}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime};~{x}~~{headLcolor]{tl}(\ell)}~~{P}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SForEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}{\ell}\downarrow\sigma=\varepsilon}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{~{x}~~{\ell}~~{P}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{\tau}\langle{},{}\rangle}\end{array}\\[27.74982pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SLoop]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{N},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\hskip 9.24994ptM=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}(x_{i}).P_{i}\hskip 9.24994pt\forall i\in I.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}\not\in\mathtt{fy}(\alpha)}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{~N~~M},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P;~N~~M},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\ \begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SLoopEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.03749pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{M},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-1.93748pt]{0.0pt}{11.62495pt}\langle{~N~~M},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

Figure 4.
Labelled transitions for processes

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SInit]}$}\\
{\langle{\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{1}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P_{n}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{\tau}\langle{(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})(P_{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\ldots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}P_{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}:\emptyset)},{\sigma[{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}\mapsto{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}]}\rangle\hskip 17.00024pt\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\not\in\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}}\end{array}\\[17.00024pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SCom_{1}]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-4.47778pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-1.72221pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{P\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\tau}\langle{P^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}\cdot\mathsf{v}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\hfill\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SCom_{2}]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-4.47778pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-1.72221pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{P\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}\mathsf{v}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\tau}\langle{P^{\prime}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\\[25.50037pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SPar]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-4.47778pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{S_{1}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S_{1}^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\hskip 17.00024pt\mathtt{bn}(\alpha)\cap\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}=\emptyset\hskip 17.00024pt\mathtt{fx}(S_{2})\cap(\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma^{\prime})}\setminus\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)})=\emptyset}{\textstyle\rule[-1.72221pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{S_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}S_{2}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}S_{2}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\\[25.50037pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SNew]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-4.47778pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{S},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\hskip 17.00024pt\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\cap\mathtt{fy}(\alpha)=\emptyset}{\textstyle\rule[-1.72221pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})S},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\hskip 17.00024pt\hfill\hskip 17.00024pt\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SStr]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-4.47778pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}S\equiv S_{1}\hskip 17.00024pt\langle{S_{1}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S_{2}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\hskip 17.00024ptS_{2}\equiv S^{\prime}}{\textstyle\rule[-1.72221pt]{0.0pt}{10.33333pt}\langle{S},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 5.
Labelled transitions for systems

We comment on the rules in
Fig.

4
and
5
.
In
Fig.

4
, rules
\mathsf{[SReq]}
and
\mathsf{[SAcc]}
deal with
the initialisation of new sessions; the store is updated to keep track
of the fresh session channels
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
used in the choreography

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
(implicitly
\alpha
-converting
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
when
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}
). Rule
\mathsf{[SSend]}
is for sending values.
Rule
\mathsf{[SRcv]}
is for receiving messages in an early style
approach (variables are assigned when firing an input prefix); the
store is updated by recording that
\mathsf{v}
is is assigned to
x
.
Rule
\mathsf{[SSeq]}
is for sequential composition.
Rules
\mathsf{[SThen]}
and
\mathsf{[SElse]}
handle conditional statements
as expected; their only peculiarity is that the guard is recorded on
the label of the transition, which is instrumental for establishing
the correspondence between systems and their types
(
cf.

Section

8
).
Rules
\mathsf{[SFor]}
,
\mathsf{[SForEnd]}
,
\mathsf{[SLoop]}
, and

\mathsf{[SLoopEnd]}
unfold the corresponding iterative process as
expected.

We now comment on the rules in
Fig.

5
.
Rule
\mathsf{[SInit]}
synchronises
n
roles with the process
\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{0}).P_{0}
initialising the session; this
creates a new session with (initially empty) queues on fresh session
names
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
.
These queues are used to exchange values as prescribed by rules

\mathsf{[SCom_{1}]}
and
\mathsf{[SCom_{2}]}
.
Communication actions of processes become silent at system level
capturing the fact that each action is performed over a session queue.
Rule
\mathsf{[SPar]}
stands for those transitions involving just some of
the components in a system.
By the condition
\mathtt{bn}(\alpha)\cap\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}=\emptyset
in the
premiss of
\mathsf{[SPar]}
,
\alpha
should contain fresh session names
when it corresponds to the creation of a new session (i.e., it is
either
\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
or

{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{p}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
).
The rightmost condition in the premiss of rule
\mathsf{[SPar]}
ensures
that each process has its own local (logical) store (i.e., there is no
confusion between bound variables of different processes).
Rule
\mathsf{[SNew]}
is standard and allows an action
\alpha
to be
observed only if it does not involve restricted names.
Rule
\mathsf{[SStr]}
is standard.

5.
Processes of the POP2 Protocol

We now present an implementation for the role
{\mathsf{{s}}}
of the global
type
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}
introduced in
Section

3
.
To ease the presentation, we abstract away from the concrete
representation of folders and use the following auxiliary abstract
operations:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{auth}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Bool}}

the authentication predicate,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{msgs}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}

maps a folder name into the number of messages in that folder,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{len}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}

maps a message index into the length of the message,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{data}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Data}}

maps a message index into its content,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{next}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}

maps a message index to the next index in the folder,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{del}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}

maps a message index to the next index in the folder after deletion.

As specified for POP2
[
6
]
, the value
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{inbox}}
of sort

{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}}
denotes the default folder.

Process
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
below gives an implementation of the role

{\mathsf{{s}}}
in the protocol POP2 described in
Section

3
.

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{s}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}

(5.1)

\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
starts by joining a session on the shared channel

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
; it plays role
{\mathsf{{s}}}
over the session channels
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}=(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{fold}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{read}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{retr}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{msg}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{acks}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ackd}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{nack}}})
.
Once the session is initiated, the continuation
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}

implements the local type
\mathsf{T}_{{\mathsf{{s}}}}
in
Section

3
.

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}.\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}~+~{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}}(c).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)\qquad\text{where}\qquad\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}\,\triangleq\,\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\,{}

(5.2)

As specified by
\mathsf{T}_{{\mathsf{{s}}}}
,
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}
waits for a message
on either
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}
or
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}
. After receiving a
message on
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}
, it sends a message on
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}

and terminates, as defined by
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}
. If the client sends
instead its credentials over channel
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}
, then the
implementation follows with
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)
:

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,~{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{auth}}\ c)}~~\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{msgs}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{inbox}})};{\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}}~~{(\overline{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}}\,{};{\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}exit}})}

(5.3)

\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)
resolves the non-deterministic choice in
{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}=\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}\ {}.{{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}}\ \oplus\ \overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}r}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}}.{{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{NMBR}$}}}
with
a conditional statement that evaluates the credentials provided by the
client. When they are valid, the implementation sends the number of
messages in the default folder
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{inbox}}
over
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}
and
proceeds as
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}
below.
On the contrary, the process closes the session after sending messages
over
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}
and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}
.

Process
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}
implements the iterative behaviour defined
by the local type
{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{NMBR}$}}
:

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,~{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{fold}}}}}(f).\overline{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{msgs}}\ f)}~+~{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{read}}}}}(m).\overline{{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{len}}\ m)};\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Size}(m)~~{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}}.\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}

(5.4)

Since
{\mathsf{{s}}}
is not the iteration-controller, its implementation
uses a repeat-until-loop in which a client can repeatedly ask for the
length of a folder (by using
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{fold}}}
) or retrieve messages
(by using
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{read}}}
) until it terminates the interaction by
sending a message over
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}
.

Processes
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Size}(m)
and
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Xfer}(m)
are the
implementations of the local types
{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{Size}$}}
and
{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{Xfer}$}}
:

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Size}(m)

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,~{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{retr}}}}.\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{msg}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{data}}\ m)};\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Xfer}(m)~+~{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{read}}}}(m).\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{len}}\ m)}~~{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{fold}}}}(f).\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{msgs}}\ f)}

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Xfer}(m)

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{acks}}}}.\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{len}}\ ({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{next}}\ m))}~+~{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ackd}}}}.\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{len}}\ ({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{del}}\ m))}~+~{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{nack}}}}.\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{len}}\ m)}

Let
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}^{\prime}
be the multiparty variant of POP2 introduced in

3.1
. The process
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}^{\prime}
below is a possible
implementation of
{\mathsf{{s}}}
in
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}^{\prime}
(i.e.,
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{{s}}}}
).

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{s}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}^{\prime}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}.\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}~+~{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}}(c).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Auth}(c)

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Auth}(c)

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,\overline{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{req}}}}}\,{c};{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{res}}}}(a).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox^{\prime}}(c,a)\

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,~{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{auth}}\ c)\land a}~~\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{msgs}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{inbox}})};{\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}}~~{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}\,{}.{\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}}}

\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}^{\prime}
is analogous to
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
in (
5.1
); we remark that
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
now includes also the
session channels
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{req}}}
and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{res}}}
used for
interacting with the authorisation authority.
After receiving the credentials of the client on session channel

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}
, the server interacts with the authorisation
authority as defined in
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Auth}(c)
: it forwards the
credentials over session channel
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{req}}}
and awaits for the
authorisation outcome
a
on session channel
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{res}}}
.
Finally,
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}^{\prime}(c,a)
resolves the non-deterministic choice
in
{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{Mbox}$}}
by taking into account both the authorisation
outcome
a
and the client’s credentials
c
. In this variant, a
client can access the inbox only if the credentials satisfy

both
the local authentication function
and
the external
authentication service.

6.
Whole-Spectrum Implementation

In this section we formally characterise the whole-spectrum
implementations of a role in a global type.
We start by introducing the notion of (candidate) implementation of a
global type, that is, a system in which each role of the global type
is implemented by a process.
The following definition syntactically characterises the processes
that can play a specific role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
in the implementation of a
global type, i.e., those processes that are able to open a session to
play role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
.

Definition 6.1

(Unique role)
.

A process
P

uniquely plays role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
if
either of the following cases holds

•

P=\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).Q
,
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(Q)}
,
and
{\mathsf{{p}}}=0

•

P={{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{p}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).Q
and

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(Q)}

•

P=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}(x_{i}).Q_{i}
and
Q_{i}
uniquely
plays role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
for each
i\in I

•

P=~{e}~~Q_{1}~~{Q_{2}}
and both
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
uniquely play
role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}

•

P=Q_{1};Q_{2}
and either
Q_{1}
uniquely plays role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
in

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(Q_{2})}
or
Q_{2}
uniquely plays
role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(Q_{1})}

•

P=~Q_{1}~~Q_{2}
and
Q_{2}
uniquely plays role
{\mathsf{{p}}}

in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(Q_{1})}
.

For technical simplicity, we require a process playing role
{\mathsf{{p}}}

in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
to open just one session over the shared channel

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
(note the restriction
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(Q)}
in the first
two items of the definition); a process playing different roles in
several instances of the same global type can be handled by using
different shared names associated to the same global type.
For branches and conditional forms we require the process to play role

{\mathsf{{p}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
regardless of the chosen branch (e.g., in every
continuation
Q_{j}
of a branching process).
The case for sequential composition is straightforward. We remark that

\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\,{e}
does not play a role in a shared name because it
cannot open any session over any shared name.
We also exclude processes like
~{x}~~{\ell}~~{Q}
, which could
potentially open several sessions of a global type (once in any
iteration of the loop). The condition for
~Q_{1}~~Q_{2}
is
analogous when requiring
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(Q_{1})}
.

To introduce the notion of implementations of a global type it is
convenient to use
contexts
, that is terms derived from the
following productions:

\mathtt{C}[\_]::=\_\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\mathtt{C}[\_]{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}S\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;S{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}\mathtt{C}[\_]\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})\mathtt{C}[\_]

Definition 6.2

(Implementation)
.

Let
\iota
be a mapping assigning a process to each

{\mathsf{{p}}}\in\{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}\}\subseteq\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}
,

P=\iota({\mathsf{{p}}}_{0})\mid\ldots\mid\iota({\mathsf{{p}}}_{n})
,

\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
a tuple of pairwise disjoint session channels in

\mathbb{Y}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\in\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}
.
A system
@
is an
\iota
-implementation of
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}

at
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
for
\{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}\}
if there is a
context

\mathtt{C}[\_]=(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{1})\cdots(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{h}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{h})(\_\mid S)
such that

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{1},\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{h}\}
,

(\{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\}\cup\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\cap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(S)}=\emptyset
, and

\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\cap\big(\bigcup_{i=1,\ldots,h}{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\big)=\emptyset

(2)

if

{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}\equiv\mathtt{C}[P]
then, for all
0\leq j\leq n
,
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}}_{j})

uniquely plays role
{\mathsf{{p}}}_{j}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}

(3)

if

{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}\equiv\mathtt{C}[(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})(P)\mid{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}]
for some
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}_{k}

then

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(P)}
.

Given a global type
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
, an

\iota
-implementation of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
at
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
is
an
\iota
-implementation at
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
for
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{G})
.

Intuitively, a system
@
is an implementation of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
if
@
is built-up from processes that implement all the
roles in
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
; the association between roles and
processes is given by the function
\iota
. In addition,
@
may
contain other processes, possibly running different sessions.
Technically, we require
@
to be written in terms of a context

\mathtt{C}[\_]=(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{1})\cdots(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{h}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{h})(\_\mid S)
, which describes the part of the system
that does not directly implement
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
.
The conditions
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{1},\ldots{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{h}\}
,

\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\cap\big(\bigcup_{i=1,\ldots,h}{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\big)=\emptyset
, and

(\{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\}\cup\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\cap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(S)}=\emptyset
ensure that
the context does not interfere with the names used for implementing

\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
.
Then, an implementation has two different shapes depending on whether
the session for
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
has been initiated or not.
Condition (
2
) stipulates that, before starting the
session, each process
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
uniquely plays the role
{\mathsf{{p}}}

in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
(i.e.,
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
is able to open a session on

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
for the role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
).
Condition (
3
) characterises the case in which the
session has been initiated, and therefore the system contains the
message queues for the initiated session.

Example 6.3
.

Consider the global type
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}
in
Section

3
and take
{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}=\iota({\mathsf{{s}}})\ |\ \iota({\mathsf{{c}}})
where
\iota
is such that

\iota({\mathsf{{s}}})=\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
and
\iota({\mathsf{{c}}})=\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C}

with
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
the process in (
5.1
)
(
cf.
page
5.1
) and
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C}\,\triangleq\,\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{1}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}\,{;}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}
.
It is easy to check that
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
uniquely plays
{\mathsf{{s}}}

in
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
while
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C}
uniquely plays
{\mathsf{{c}}}
(after
assuming that
{\mathsf{{c}}}
is
{\mathsf{{0}}}
).
Hence, it is straightforward that
@
is a

\iota
-implementation
of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})={\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}
at

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
(it is enough to consider the identity context
\mathtt{C}[\_]=\_
).

Consider now a more involved situation in which a process

\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime}}
that implements
{\mathsf{{c}}}
also interacts with another
process
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}D}
over a different session, e.g.,

\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime}}\,\triangleq\,{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{0}}^{{\mathsf{{a}}}}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}).\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{1}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}\,{;}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}.\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime\prime}}\qquad\qquad\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}D}\,\triangleq\,\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{0}}^{{\mathsf{{1}}}}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}D^{\prime}}

In this case,
\iota^{\prime}
is such that
\iota^{\prime}({\mathsf{{s}}})=\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
and
\iota^{\prime}({\mathsf{{c}}})=C^{\prime}
. Then,
{{}{\iota^{\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}=\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}\ |\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C}\ |\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}D}
is an

\iota^{\prime}
-implementation
of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
at
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
(it
suffices to consider the context
\mathtt{C}[\_]=\_\ |\ D
). Note that

{{}{\iota^{\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}\xrightarrow{\tau}(\nu{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{0})(\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}\ |\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime\prime\prime}}\ |\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}D^{\prime}}\ |\ {\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]})={{}{\iota^{\prime\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}

with
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime\prime\prime}}\ \,\triangleq\,\ \overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{1}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}\,{;}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}.\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime\prime}}
and
\iota^{\prime\prime}=\iota^{\prime}[{\mathsf{{c}}}\mapsto\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime\prime\prime}}]
. Then, we can conclude
that
{{}{\iota^{\prime\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}
is an
\iota^{\prime\prime}
-implementation of

\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
at
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
by considering the context
\mathtt{C}[\_]=(\nu{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{0})(\_|\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}D^{\prime}}\ |\ {\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]})
.

Consider now the transition

{{}{\iota^{\prime\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}\xrightarrow{\tau}(\nu{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}_{0})((\nu{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})(\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}\ |\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\ |\ {\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}[]\ldots[]{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]})\ |\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}D^{\prime}}\ |\ {{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}z}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]})={{}{\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}

where
\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}({\mathsf{{s}}})=\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}
and
\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}({\mathsf{{c}}})=C^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}
. In this case
{{}{\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}
is an

\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}
-implementation of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
at
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
; the
sub-term

(\nu{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})(\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}\ |\ \mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}C^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\ |\ {\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}[]\ldots[]{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]})

stands for the session corresponding to the global type
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
, while the context represents the rest of the system.

\diamond

We characterise WSI as a relation between the execution traces of a
global type
\mathcal{G}
and its implementations
@
.
An execution trace of a system
@
is a sequence of events of the
form
\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}}\rangle
and

\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}}\rangle
, which respectively represent
an output and an input action performed by
{\mathsf{{p}}}
over the channel

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[REnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\forall{\mathsf{{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{G})\;\colon\;\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})=\qquad\forall\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\;\colon\;\text{ queue on }\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\text{ is empty in }{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\epsilon\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RSnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}\langle{\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}}\langle{P},{\sigma}\rangle\hfill\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\hfill{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\\[5.0pt]
\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\tau}\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{\sigma}\rangle\qquad\qquad\qquad r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}}}},{\sigma}\rangle\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\rangle r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RRcv]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}\langle{\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}}\langle{P},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\hfill\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\hfill{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\\[5.0pt]
\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\tau}\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\qquad\qquad\qquad r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}}}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\rangle r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RExt_{1}]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}\langle{\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\qquad\mathtt{n}(\alpha)\cap\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}=\emptyset\qquad\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\beta}\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}}}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\\[5.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(\beta)}\qquad r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}}}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RExt_{2}]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\beta}\langle{({{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}})^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\qquad\qquad r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{({{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}})^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[ROpen]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}\langle{\iota({\mathsf{{p}}}_{0})},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e_{0}\vdash\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})}\langle{P_{0}},{\sigma_{0}}\rangle\qquad\qquad\forall 1\leq i\leq n\;\colon\;\langle{\iota({\mathsf{{p}}}_{i})},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e_{i}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{i}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})}\langle{P_{i}},{\sigma_{i}}\rangle\\[5.0pt]
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}))=\{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{0},{\mathsf{{p}}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}\}\hfill\iota^{\prime}=\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{0}}\mapsto{P_{0}}]\ldots[{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}}\mapsto{P_{n}}]\\[5.0pt]
\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\tau}\langle{{{}{\iota^{\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}}}},{\sigma[{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}\mapsto{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}]}\rangle\hfill r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{\iota^{\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}}}},{\sigma[{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}\mapsto{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}]}\rangle\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle}\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 6.
Runs of implementations

Definition 6.4

(Runs of implementations)
.

Let
@
be an
\iota
-implementation of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
.
The set of
runs of
@
initiated on
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
with store

\sigma
, written
\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle
, is the set
inductively defined by the rules in
Fig.

6
.
We write
\mathcal{R}{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}
for
\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{\emptyset}\rangle

and extend the notion to sets of implementations
\mathbb{I}
as

\mathcal{R}\mathbb{I}=\cup_{I\in\mathbb{I}}\mathcal{R}I
.

Rules in
Fig.

6
rely on the semantics of

Fig.

4
and
Fig.

5
. Rule
\mathsf{[REnd]}

establishes that a completed session, i.e., one in which all processes
are terminated and the session queues are empty, contains the empty
run
\epsilon
.
Non-empty runs of
@
are defined in terms of the input and output
actions that processes
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
perform over the session
channels
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
, as described by the rules
\mathsf{[RSnd]}

and
\mathsf{[RRcv]}
.
In rule
\mathsf{[RSnd]}
,
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
performs an output over a
session channel associated with
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
; which is formally captured
by the conditions

\langle{\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}}\langle{P},{\sigma}\rangle
and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
in the premiss.
When
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
evolves to
P
by performing

\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}
,
@
evolves to
{{}{\iota^{\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}
with

\iota^{\prime}=\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]
, i.e.,
\iota^{\prime}
coincides with
\iota

in all roles but
{{\mathsf{{p}}}}
. This is stated by the condition

\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\tau}\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{\sigma}\rangle
in the premiss of
the rule.
Hence,
@
contains a run
\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\rangle r

(see the conclusion of the rule) when
r
is a run of the state

\langle{{{}{\iota[{{\mathsf{{p}}}}\mapsto{P}]}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle
reached after

\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
performs
\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}
.
We remark that runs abstract away from the particular values sent by
the processes and keep instead the sorts of sent value (i.e.,
condition
\mathsf{v}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
).
Input events are handled analogously in rule
\mathsf{[RRcv]}
; in this
case also
\sigma
evolves to
\sigma^{\prime}
when
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
performs
an input.

Rule
\mathsf{[RExt_{1}]}
accounts for the computation steps of
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
that do not involve session channels in
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
(condition

\mathtt{n}(\alpha)\cap\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}=\emptyset
), which can be an
internal transition
\tau
in a role, a communication over a channel
not in
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
, or a session initiation.
This rule allows each process to freely initiate a session that does
not correspond to the global type
\mathsf{G}
, i.e., over a shared name
different from
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
(condition
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(\beta)}
).
Rule
\mathsf{[RExt_{2}]}
handles the cases in which the transition of

@
does not involve any process
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})
. This is captured
by the fact that the continuation
{{}{\iota^{\prime}}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}
uses the same
mapping
\iota
. By the definition of
@
, the reduction does not
interfere with the names of the session, i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(\beta)}\cap(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\})
holds.

Rule
\mathsf{[ROpen]}
allows for the initiation of a new session on

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
and requires all roles to participate in the synchronisation
(as stated by the three first premisses).
We assume that any role in the implementation will execute exactly one
action over the channel
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
which also matches the role assigned
by
\iota
.
Nested sessions are handled by assuming that all sessions are created
over different channels that have the same type.
This is just a technical simplification analogous to the possibility
of having annotations to indicate the particular instance of the
session under analysis.

The runs of an implementation abstractly capture the traces of
communications of the processes in the system.
This can be easily formalised by using a more concrete relation on
systems.
More precisely, we define
\xhookrightarrow{\_}
as the relation induced by the
rules such as those in
Fig.

4
once the
\tau
in the
conclusion of rules
\mathsf{[SCom_{1}]}
and
\mathsf{[SCom_{2}]}
is replaced
with the output and input action respectively.
Then we can state the following proposition:

Theorem 6.5
.

Given an implementation
@
and a store
\sigma
, if
r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle
is a run of length
m
then there is a
sequence
{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}\xhookrightarrow{\alpha_{1}}\langle{S_{1}},{\sigma_{1}}\rangle\cdots\xhookrightarrow{\alpha_{n}}\langle{S_{n}},{\sigma_{n}}\rangle
such that we can find an
order preserving bijection
\chi
between the sets
\{1,\ldots,m\}

and
\{1\leq j\leq n\;\big|\;\alpha_{i}\text{ not a }\tau\}
such
that the
i
-th element in
r
is an input of sort
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
iff so is
the
\alpha_{\chi(i)}
and the value in
\alpha_{\chi(i)}
has the
sort
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
.

Proof.

Straightforward induction on the derivation of
r\in\mathcal{R}\langle{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}},{}\rangle
.
∎

We now introduce the notion of runs associated to a global type. Our
notion of WSI will allow us to implement an iterative type, which
accounts for an unbounded number of repetitions, with a process
exhibiting a bounded number of iterations. For this reason, we deviate
from the previous definition of traces of global
types
[
14
,
10
,
25
,
24
]
and
use
annotated traces
to distinguish mandatory from optional
events.
Annotating optional events is instrumental to the comparison of traces
of iterative types (which is defined below).
Syntactically, an optional sequence
r
of events is written
[r]
.
As usual, we consider an asynchronous communication model and a trace
implicitly denotes the equivalence class of all traces obtained by
permuting causally independent events, that is events executed by
different participants on different channels.

Definition 6.6

(Runs of a global type)
.

Given a global type term
\mathsf{G}
, the set
\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})
denotes the
runs allowed by
\mathsf{G}
and is defined as the least set closed under
the rules in
Fig.

7
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RGEnd]}$}\\
{\epsilon\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{end})}\end{array}\hfill\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RGCom]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}j\in I\qquad r\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G}_{j})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{j}}\rangle\langle{{\mathsf{{q}}}_{j}},{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{j}}\rangle r\in\mathcal{R}(\sum_{\in}{\mathsf{{}}}\rightarrowtriangle{\mathsf{{}}}\colon\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{{}_{\mathit{}}}};)}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RGSeq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G}_{1})\quad r_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G}_{2})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}r_{2}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G}_{1};\mathsf{G}_{2})}\end{array}\quad\ \ \begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RG^{*}_{1}]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})}\end{array}\hfill\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RG^{*}_{2}]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})\quad r_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}[r_{2}]\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RGIter]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})\quad\mathtt{rdy}(\mathsf{G})=\{{\mathsf{{p}}}\}\quad\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{G})=\{{\mathsf{{p}}},{\mathsf{{p}}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}\}\quad\forall 1\leq i\leq n\;\colon\;f({\mathsf{{p}}}_{i})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{1}}\rangle\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{1}},{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{1}}\rangle\ldots\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{n}}\rangle\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}},{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{n}}\rangle\quad\in\quad\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})}\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 7.
Runs of a global type

The first three rules of
Fig.

7
are
straightforward.
The runs of an iterative type
\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}}
are given by the
rule
\mathsf{[RGIter]}
, whose premiss uses the set

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})
to unfold
\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}}
(as defined by
the rules
\mathsf{[RG^{*}_{1}]}
and
\mathsf{[RG^{*}_{2}]}
).
Optional events are introduced when unfolding an iterative type (rule

\mathsf{[RG^{*}_{2}]}
). The main motivation is that an iterative type

\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}}
denotes an unbounded number of repetitions of (traces
of)
{\mathsf{G}}
(i.e., an infinite number of traces). Note that

{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})}=\{r_{1},r_{1}[r_{2}],r_{1}[r_{2}[r_{3}]],\ldots\}
with
r_{i}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})
.
Rule
\mathsf{[RGIter]}
adds the events associated to the termination of
an iteration:
(i)
the ready role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
sends the termination
signal to any other role by using the dedicated channels specified by

f
(i.e.,
\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{1}}\rangle,\ldots,\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{n}}\rangle
), and
(ii)
all
roles but the ready one receive the corresponding termination message
(i.e.,
\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{1}},{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{1}}\rangle,\ldots,\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}},{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{n}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{n}}\rangle
).
We just consider one of the possible interleavings of termination
events because we consider traces up-to the permutation of causally
independent events.

Definition 6.7

(Trace preorder)
.

The
trace preorder

\lessdot
is the least preorder on
annotated traces satisfying the following axioms and rules.

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[\lessdot drop]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}-}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}[r]\lessdot\epsilon}\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[\lessdot emp]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}-}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\epsilon\lessdot r}\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[\lessdot cmp]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}\lessdot r_{1}^{\prime}\quad r_{2}\lessdot r_{2}^{\prime}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}r_{2}\lessdot r_{1}^{\prime}r_{2}^{\prime}}\end{array}

We say
r^{\prime}

covers

r
when
r\lessdot r^{\prime}
, i.e., when
r^{\prime}

matches all mandatory actions of
r
.
Analogously, we say a set
R_{2}
of annotated traces
covers

another set
R_{1}
, written
R_{1}\Subset R_{2}
, if for all
r\in R_{1}

there is
r^{\prime}\in R_{2}
such that
r\lessdot r^{\prime}
.

Definition 6.8

(Whole-spectrum implementation)
.

A set
\mathbb{I}
of implementations
covers
a global type

{}({})\,\triangleq\,{}
if
\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})\Subset\mathcal{R}\mathbb{I}
.
A process
P
is a
whole-spectrum implementation of
{\mathsf{{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{G})
when there exists a set
\mathbb{I}
of

\iota
-implementations of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
at
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
that
covers
\mathsf{G}
such that
{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}\in\mathbb{I}
implies
\iota({\mathsf{{p}}})=P
.

A whole-spectrum implementation (WSI) of a role
{\mathsf{{p}}}
is a process

P
such that any expected behaviour of the global type can be
obtained by putting
P
into a proper context.
For iteration types, the comparison of annotated traces implies that
the implementation has to be able to perform the iteration body at
least once, but it can arbitrarily choose the number of iterations.

Remark 9

(Trace semantics for WSI)
.

WSI is based on a trace semantics. WSI implementations are
deterministic programs, hence their behaviour if faithfully captured
by the set of their execution traces. A stronger notion like
bisimulation is not necessary. In fact, the branching specified in a
global type becomes deterministic in a process, therefore the set of
all possible executions of a process (in a given implementation)
results in a single trace.

7.
Typing

In this section we introduce a typing discipline to guarantee that a
well-typed process is a WSI of the role it plays in a global type.
Technically we rely on an enriched version of local types, dubbed

pseudo-types
, that takes into account branching enabling
conditions.

7.1.
Pseudo-types & Typing judgements

The scaffolding of our typing discipline is standard but for the need
of making the typing depending on the expressions the processes use to
render choices.
This requires to revisit the usual definition of mergeability
(cf.
1
below) that now relies on a notion of

normalisation
of local types.

The syntax of pseudo-types is given by the following grammar:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}::={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\quad{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\quad{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\quad{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star}\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}

We call
guards
the expressions
e_{i}
occurring in a
pseudo-type.
Guards keep track of the conditions that have to be satisfied in order
to enable a certain behaviour.
For instance, the pseudo-type

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{\oplus}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in\{1,2\}}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{int}}}.\mathsf{end}}}}

where the guards are
e_{1}=x>0
and
e_{2}=x\leq 0
.
By
e_{1}
,
x
needs to be strictly positive in order to choose the
first branch.
Local types from
Section

2
can be thought of as pseudo-types
where all guards are
\mathtt{true}
. Hereafter we may omit guards
\mathtt{true}

and have e.g.,

{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\text{ abbreviated as }\mathsf{end}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\text{ abbreviated as }\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}

The notions of free and bound names straightforwardly extend to
pseudo-types.
We will write
\mathrm{var}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
for the set of variables occurring in the
expressions of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathtt{fy}({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})
for the set of session channels
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
; for instance,
\mathrm{var}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{\oplus}})=\{x\}
.

\begin{array}[]{lrcl@{\hspace{-1.3cm}}r}(1)&\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&=&{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\[9.24994pt]
\lx@intercol\text{let }J=\{i\in I\;\big|\;(e\land e_{i})\iff\mathtt{false}\}\text{ in the clauses $(2)$ and $(3)$ below}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[9.24994pt]
(2)&\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}})&=&\begin{cases}{\mathtt{false}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}&\textit{if}\ \ I=J\\
{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus J}{e_{i}\land e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\land e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}})}}}&\textit{if}\ \ I\neq J\end{cases}\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\
(3)&\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}})&=&\begin{cases}{\mathtt{false}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}&\textit{if}\ \ I=J\\
{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I\setminus J}{e_{i}\land e}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\land e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}})}}}&\textit{if}\ \ I\neq J\end{cases}\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\[18.49988pt]
(4)&\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&=&\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\
(5)&\mathsf{nf}({e,\big({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\big);{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&=&\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})}}}})\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\
(6)&\mathsf{nf}({e,\big({\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\big);{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&=&\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})}}}})\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\
(7)&\mathsf{nf}({e,({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime});{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}})&=&\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime})}})\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\
(8)&\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&=&\begin{cases}{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}&\textit{if}\ \mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})={e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\\
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{if}\ \mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})\neq{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\end{cases}\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\\
(9)&\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star}})&=&\begin{cases}{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}&\textit{if}\ \mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})={e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\\
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})^{\star}&\textit{if}\ \mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})\neq{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\end{cases}\hfil\hskip-36.98866pt\end{array}

Figure 8.
Normalisation procedure for pseudo-types

Given a pseudo-type
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
, the
normal form of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
, written

\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
, is defined as
\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})=\mathsf{nf}({\mathtt{true},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
where

\mathsf{nf}({\_,\_})
given by the equations in

Fig.

8
.
Intuitively, the normalisation of a pseudo-type propagates the guards
of branches to their continuations while removing those alternatives
with inconsistent guards.
We just remark that
\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
is defined for any
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
(details
are in
Section

A.1
).

The notion of normalisation is instrumental to adapt the standard
merge operation
\_{\bowtie}\_
of session
types
[
10
]
to pseudo-types.
Our definition of
\_{\bowtie}\_
requires the
mergeability
of
pseudo-types, which amounts to have branches with the same
communication prefix guarded by mutually exclusive conditions.

Definition 1

(Mergeable pseudo-types)
.

Two pseudo-types
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
in normal form are

mergeable
, if

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}^{\prime}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}}}}
and for
all
i\in I
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}
are mergeable,
and
e_{i}\land e_{i}^{\prime}\iff\mathtt{false}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\cup J}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\cup K}{e_{i}^{\prime}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}}}}
with
I\cap J=I\cap K=\emptyset
and sets

{\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}\}}_{i\in I}
,
{\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}\}}_{j\in J}
, and

{\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{k}\}}_{k\in K}
pairwise disjoint, and for all
i\in I
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}
are mergeable,
and
e_{i}\land e_{i}^{\prime}\iff\mathtt{false}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2}
as well as
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
mergeable

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}=({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1})^{\star}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}=({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2})^{\star}

with
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2}
mergeable.

Basically,
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
are mergeable when they have the same
structure and at choice points branches either use different channels
or they use mutually exclusive guards.
When types are mergeable, operation
\_{\bowtie}\_
“glues” branches that use the same channel.

Definition 2

(Merge)
.

The
merge
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
of two mergeable
pseudo-types
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
is defined as:

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}=\begin{cases}{e\lor e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}&\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\\[5.0pt]
{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}\lor e_{i}^{\prime}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}}}}&\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}^{\prime}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}}}}\\[5.0pt]
{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\cup J}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}&\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{j\in J}{e_{j}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{j}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{j}}}}\\[5.0pt]
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2});({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{2})&\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\\[5.0pt]
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2})^{\star}&\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}=({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{1})^{\star}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}=({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{2})^{\star}\end{cases}

As we will see, our typing discipline keeps track of the assumptions
(i.e., the guards) necessary to reach a particular point in the
processes.
In fact, systems are typed by judgements of the form

\displaystyle{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}

(7.1)

stipulating that, under the assumption
e
and the type assignment of
variables
\Gamma
, the system
S
is typed as
\Delta
.
In (
7.1
),
\Gamma
and
\Delta
are (possibly empty) partial
functions.
We adopt the usual syntactic notation for environments:

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\emptyset\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\quad\Gamma,x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle::=

\displaystyle\emptyset\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\quad\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}:\mathcal{G}\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\quad\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}\quad\;\;\ \big|\ \;\;\quad\Delta,\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}

Environments
\Gamma
assign sorts
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
to variables
x
.
Environments
\Delta
, called
specifications
, map (
i
) shared
names
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
to global types
\mathcal{G}
, (
ii
) participants’ sessions

{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
to pseudo-types
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
, and (
iii
) session names

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}
to queues of sorts
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
.
As usual, we implicitly assume that in a judgement of the form (
7.1
) the following holds:

•

x\not\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Gamma)}
when writing
\Gamma,x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
,
and

•

{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}\not\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
when writing

\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}:\mathcal{G}
(likewise for participants’ sessions and for
sessions’ queues).

For judgements of the form (
7.1
) we also assume that

•

\mathtt{fx}(e)\subseteq\mathrm{dom}{(\Gamma)}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathtt{fn}(S)}\subseteq\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
, and

•

in
\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
it is

\mathtt{fy}({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})\subseteq\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
and

\displaystyle\forall{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})},{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{2}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)},\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{1}\cap\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{2}\neq\emptyset\implies\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{1}=\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{2}

(7.2)

Condition (
7.2
) states that a session channel can be used
only in one session.
We sometimes write
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
when there exists

{\mathsf{{p}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
.
Similarly,
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
stands for
\exists\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}\;\colon\;\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
.
The extension of
\mathrm{var}({\_})
and
\mathtt{fy}()
to environments is
straightforward.
