Summary of operations and predicates for environments

Our typing relies on
an operation that recovers types from pseudo-types by removing guards. Formally,
guard removal
\Game\big({\cdot}\big)
is defined as

\begin{array}[]{c}\Game\big({{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}}\big)=\mathsf{end}\qquad\qquad\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\big)=\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}\big);\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}\big)\qquad\qquad\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star}}\big)=\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}\big)^{\star}\\[10.0pt]
\Game\big({{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}}\big)=\displaystyle{\sum_{\alpha\in I/_{\sim}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{\alpha}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\alpha}}.\Game\big({{\bowtie}_{i\in\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}\big)}\\[10.0pt]
\Game\big({{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}}\big)=\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{\alpha\in I/_{\sim}}\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{\alpha}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\alpha}}.\Game\big({{\bowtie}_{i\in\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}\big)}\end{array}

where in the last two equations
\sim
is the equivalence relation on

I
defined as
i\sim j\iff\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}
and

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\alpha}=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}
for
i\in\alpha
.
Other auxiliary operations and predicates on environments are listed
in
Fig.

9
and formally defined in the following
section.

7.2.
Typing rules

The typing rules for processes and systems are grouped in

Fig.

10
and
Fig.

11
.
For the sake of readability, we restate the typing rules as we comment
them so to introduce notation and concepts appearing in the rules as
we present them.

To type a request for a new session
\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P
we use the following rule

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VReq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\Delta({\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})\equiv\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{0}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}\qquad\Game\big({\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})}\big)=\mathsf{nf}({\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{0}}}}})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}

The premiss checks that the continuation
P
can be typed with

\Delta
extended with an assignment of the pseudo-type
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
to the
participant’s session
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
, for some
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}

matching
the projection of the global type
\Delta({\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})
on
the corresponding role.
Intuitively, the type obtained by removing guards from
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}

coincides with the projection of the global type.

The rule
\mathsf{[VAcc]}
for typing the acceptance for the
{\mathsf{{p}}}
-th
role
{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{p}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P
is defined analogously.

An external choice is checked by

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VRcv]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\forall i\in I\;\colon\;\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma,x_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}\ \vdash\ {P_{i}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}(x_{i}).P_{i}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\end{array}

that types each branch
P_{i}
against the respective continuation of
the type
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}
(once
\Gamma
is extended with
the type assignment on the bound name
x_{i}
); the first condition in
the premiss permits to branch only over a subset of the session
channels.

An output
\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\,{e^{\prime}}
and the idle process are respectively
typed as follows

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VSend]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{\Gamma}\ {}\ {e^{\prime}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\qquad\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\qquad\Delta\text{ $\mathsf{end}$-only}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\,{e^{\prime}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}};{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}}}\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\Delta\text{ $\mathsf{end}$-only}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}

The expression
e^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{[VSend]}
has to be of the sort expected
on channel
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}
; moreover, no further actions should occur on
session channels (rendered with the condition
\Delta

\mathsf{end}
-only
abbreviating
\forall{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{q}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}\;\colon\;\mathsf{nf}({\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{q}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}})={e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
);
for the idle process we simply require
\Delta
to map each session
channel to the
\mathsf{end}
type.

The typing of sequential compositions is handled by the rule

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VSeq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{1}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{1};P_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1};\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}

that requires to decompose the specification into
\Delta_{1}
and

\Delta_{2}
to respectively type each part of the sequential
composition.
In the conclusion of the rule, the partial operation

\_;\_
on specifications requires that

\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{2})}\subseteq\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1})}
and

\Delta_{1}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}=\Delta_{2}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}

and it is defined as follows

\begin{array}[]{rcl}(\Delta_{1};\Delta_{2})|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}&=&\Delta_{1}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}\\
(\Delta_{1};\Delta_{2}){\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}&=&\begin{cases}\Delta_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})};\Delta_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}&{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{2})}\\
\Delta_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}&{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1})}\setminus\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{2})}\\
\text{undef}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

The notion of mergeable pseudo-types is extended to specification
pairs in order to type conditionals.
Specifications
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
are
mergeable
when the
local types they assign to sessions are mergeable.
Formally,
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
are mergeable iff

\Delta_{1}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}=\Delta_{2}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}
,

\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1})}=\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{2})}
and, for all
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1})}
,
\Delta_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
and
\Delta_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
are mergeable.
When
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
are mergeable,
\Delta_{1}{\bowtie}\Delta_{2}
merges the local types of sessions:

(\Delta_{1}{\bowtie}\Delta_{2})|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}=\Delta_{1}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad(\Delta_{1}{\bowtie}\Delta_{2}){\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}=\Delta_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}{\bowtie}\Delta_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}

We remark that the merge operation on specifications is idempotent,
associative, and commutative.
For conditionals we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[Vif]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {P_{1}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e\land\neg e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {P_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~{e^{\prime}}~~P_{1}~~{P_{2}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}{\bowtie}\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}

that requires to decompose the specification into two specifications

\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
so to type conditional processes with the
merge (
cf.

2
) of
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
.
The premiss checks that the then-branch is typed in
\Delta_{1}
after
extending the assumption
e
with the guard
e^{\prime}
of the conditional
while the else-branch is typed in
\Delta_{2}
after extending
e
with
the negation of
e^{\prime}
.
Recall that judgements require consistency of their assumptions, hence
rule
\mathsf{[Vif]}
is not applicable if
e\land e^{\prime}
or
e\land\neg e^{\prime}
are inconsistent.

Example 3
.

Our typing distinguishes between
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
in
Section

1

because
B_{1}
is validated while
B_{2}
is not.
This is due to the rule
\mathsf{[VIf]}
.
In fact, after a few verification steps on
B_{1}
we can apply rule

\mathsf{[VIf]}
and prove the following judgement:

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~{({check}\ c)}~~\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ok}}}}\,{}~~{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ko}}}}\,{}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}

where
\Gamma
assigns some sort to
\mathsf{c}
and

\Delta=((\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{login}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{deposit}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{overdraft}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ok}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ko}}}),{\mathsf{{b}}}):\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ok}}}}\ {}\oplus\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ko}}}}\ {}
.
Instead, for
B_{2}
we would have to prove

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ko}}}}\,{}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}

which makes the validation of
B_{2}
fail; this is due to the fact
that the only rule for typing a sending process is
\mathsf{[VSend]}
,
which cannot be applied against the specification
\Delta
that
assigns
\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ok}}}}\ {}\oplus\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{ko}}}}\ {}
to
the participant’s session.

\diamond

For-loops are typed with the following two rules

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VForEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\land{\ell=\varepsilon}\ \not\vdash\ \bot\qquad{\Gamma}\ {}\ {\ell}{}{[{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}]\qquad\Delta\text{ $\mathsf{end}$-only}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~{x}~~{\ell}~~{P}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VFor]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e}\land{\ell\not=\varepsilon}\ \not\vdash\ \bot\quad{\Gamma}\ {}\ {\ell}{}{[{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}]\quad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma,x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}\quad\Delta\mbox{ active}\quad x\not\in\mathrm{var}({\Delta})\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~{x}~~{\ell}~~{P}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\star}}}\end{array}\end{array}

Rule
\mathsf{[VForEnd]}
handles the case in which the expression
\ell

denotes an empty list, that is when the for-loop should be skipped.
For this reason, the typing is similar to the typing of the idle
process (rule
\mathsf{[VEnd]}
).
When the expression
\ell
denotes a non-empty list under the
assumption
e
(i.e.,
{\Gamma}\ {}\ {\ell}{}{[{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}]
and

{e}\land{\ell\not=\varepsilon}\ \not\vdash\ \bot
), we apply rule

\mathsf{[VFor]}
to validate for-loops.
The conclusion of the rule types the for-loop with
\Delta^{\star}

which introduces iterative pseudo-types and is defined as follows:

\Delta^{\star}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}=\Delta|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Delta^{\star}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}=(\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})})^{\star}

Note that
\Delta
has to type the body
P
under the context
\Gamma

extended with
x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
because
x
can occur free in
P
.
Moreover,
P
has to inform all other peers that the iteration
continues.
This is checked by the condition
\Delta

active
, namely that
for all
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
,
\mathsf{nf}({\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}})={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
.
Condition
x\not\in\mathrm{var}({\Delta})
ensures that guards of
P
do not
depend on the iteration variable
x
, making each choice available at
each iteration.

Rule
\mathsf{[VLoop]}
below types passive processes of iterations.

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VLoop]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {N}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {M}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}\qquad\Delta_{1}\mbox{ and }\Delta_{2}\mbox{ passively compatible}\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~N~~M}\ \triangleright\ {{\Delta_{1}^{\star}};\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}

The premiss of the rule types the iteration body
N
and the loop exit

M
with specifications
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
respectively.
Both specifications are required to be
passively compatible
,
i.e., there is just one session that decides whether to continue or
terminate the iteration, and use different channels to communicate
such choice. Formally, specifications
\Delta_{1}=\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
and
\Delta_{2}=\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
are
passively compatible
iff

\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}\subseteq{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}\cup\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}},\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}={\displaystyle{\sum_{j\in J}{e_{j}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{j}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{j}}}}

with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
for all
i\in I,j\in J
.

We now consider the typing rules for systems in
Fig.

11
,
which essentially deal with parallel composition, restriction of
shared names, and queues.
For parallel composition

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VPar]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e_{1}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {S_{1}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e_{2}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {S_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}\qquad\Delta_{1}\text{ and }\Delta_{2}\text{ independent}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e_{1}\land e_{2}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {S_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}S_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}

requires to split the specification into two
independent

specifications
\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
that respectively type each
side of the parallel.
Specifications are independent when they agree on shared names and are
disjoint on queues and participants’ sessions; more precisely,

\Delta_{1}
and
\Delta_{2}
are
independent
when

•

\Delta_{1}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}}=\Delta_{2}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}}
and

\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}})}\cap\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{2}|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}})}=\emptyset

•

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1})}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{2})}
, if
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\cap\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
then
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}=\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}^{\prime}
and

{\mathsf{{p}}}\neq{\mathsf{{p^{\prime}}}}

The union of independent specifications enjoys the sanity
condition (
7.2
).

A restricted session is typed by

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VNew]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta|_{-\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}}}\end{array}

that removes participants’ sessions and sessions’ queues referring to
the restricted names
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
from the specification
\Delta

typing the scope
S
(this restricted specification is denoted as

\Delta|_{-\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}
).

The typing of queues is straightforwardly handled by the following two
rules

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VQueue]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}\vec{\mathsf{v}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}\ \triangleright\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}\vec{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}\cdot\mathsf{v}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}\ \triangleright\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}\vec{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VEmpty]}$}\\
{{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}\ \triangleright\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}}\end{array}

where
\_\cdot\_
denotes the concatenation operation on sequences
and
\mathsf{[VEmpty]}
permits to type empty queues.

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VReq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\Delta({\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})\equiv\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{0}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}\qquad\Game\big({\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})}\big)=\mathsf{nf}({\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{0}}}}})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VAcc]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\Delta({\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})\equiv\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}\qquad\Game\big({\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})}\big)=\mathsf{nf}({\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{p}}}}})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{p}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VRcv]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\forall i\in I\;\colon\;\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma,x_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}\ \vdash\ {P_{i}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}(x_{i}).P_{i}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VSend]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{\Gamma}\ {}\ {e^{\prime}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\qquad\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\qquad\Delta\text{ $\mathsf{end}$-only}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\,{e^{\prime}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}};{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}}}\end{array}\hfill\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\Delta\text{ $\mathsf{end}$-only}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VSeq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{1}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{1};P_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1};\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VIf]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {P_{1}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e\land\neg e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {P_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~{e^{\prime}}~~P_{1}~~{P_{2}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}{\bowtie}\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VForEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\land{\ell=\varepsilon}\ \not\vdash\ \bot\qquad{\Gamma}\ {}\ {\ell}{}{[{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}]\qquad\Delta\text{ $\mathsf{end}$-only}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~{x}~~{\ell}~~{P}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VFor]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e}\land{\ell\not=\varepsilon}\ \not\vdash\ \bot\quad{\Gamma}\ {}\ {\ell}{}{[{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}]\quad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma,x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}\quad\Delta\mbox{ active}\quad x\not\in\mathrm{var}({\Delta})\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~{x}~~{\ell}~~{P}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\star}}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VLoop]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {N}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {M}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}\qquad\Delta_{1}\mbox{ and }\Delta_{2}\mbox{ passively compatible}\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~N~~M}\ \triangleright\ {{\Delta_{1}^{\star}};\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}\end{array}

Typing rules for processes

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VPar]}$}\\

{{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}\ \triangleright\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}}\end{array}\\[5.0pt]

Typing rules for systems

7.3.
Typing the POP2 Protocol

We now apply our type system to the implementations of our running
example.
We start by considering the process
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
in
5.1
(page
5.1
) and the specification
\Delta={\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}:{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}
with
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}
from
Section

3
. We recall that
its projection on
{\mathsf{{s}}}
is
{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}={{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{s}}}}
(also from
3
).
Then, the typing judgement for
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init}
is obtained by using
rule
\mathsf{[VAcc]}
as follows

\vdots

\Delta(u)\equiv{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}\quad{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\emptyset}\ \vdash\ {\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}}\quad\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}

\mathsf{[VAcc]}

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\emptyset}\ \vdash\ {{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{s}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}

where the continuation
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}
is typed against the
specification
\Delta
extended with a new participant’s session

{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
whose type
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}
matches
{\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}
, i.e.,
\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}
.
Such
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}
is obtained from the judgement

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\emptyset}\ \vdash\ {\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}}
.
Since

\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Srv}\,\triangleq\,{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}.\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}\ +\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}}(c).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)

is an input-guarded process, the judgement is obtained by applying
rule
\mathsf{[VRcv]}
as follows:

\vdots

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\emptyset}\ \vdash\ {\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}}}

\vdots

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {c:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}\ \vdash\ {\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}}

\mathsf{[VRcv]}

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\emptyset}\ \vdash\ {{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}.\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}+{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}}(c).\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}}

(7.3)

where

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}\ {}}.{{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}}\ +\ {\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}

with
\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}
and

\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}
so to satisfy

\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}
.
The first premiss in (
7.3
) is derived as follows
by taking

{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}={\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\ {}}.{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}

(recall that
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}\,\triangleq\,\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\,{}
and

\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\,{}
is a shorthand for

\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\,{()}
).

\emptyset\vdash():{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Unit}}\qquad\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}\in\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\quad\qquad(\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}})
end-only

\mathsf{[VSend]}

{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\emptyset}\ \vdash\ {\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\,{()}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}}}

The second premiss in (

) follows by using rule

\mathsf{[VIf]}
because
\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)
in (

) is a
conditional process (hereafter, we write
e
as shorthand for

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{auth}}\ c
).

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{c}\vdots\\
\hline\cr{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {c:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}\ \vdash\ {\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{mn}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{inbox}})};{\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then}$}}}\end{array}\\[-15.0pt]
\hskip 170.71652pt\begin{array}[]{c}\vdots\\
\hline\cr{\neg e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {c:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}\ \vdash\ {\overline{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}}\,{};{\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Exit}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{else}$}}}\end{array}\end{array}

\mathsf{[VIf]}

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {c:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}\ \vdash\ {\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Mbox}(c)}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}}}\end{array}

with

.
The second premiss above can be shown by using the rules

\mathsf{[VSeq]}
and
\mathsf{[VSend]}
and by taking

{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{else}$}}={\neg e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}\ {}};{\neg e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\neg e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\ {}};{\neg e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}

For the first premiss in (

) we use rule
\mathsf{[Seq]}
as follows

\vdots

{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {c:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}\ \vdash\ {\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{mn}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{inbox}})}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{1}}$}}}}

\mathsf{[VSeq]}

{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {c:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}\ \vdash\ {\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\,{({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{mn}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{inbox}})};{\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then}$}}}

(7.9)

with
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then}$}}={{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{1}}$}};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{2}}$}}
.
By applying
\mathsf{[VSend]}
, we conclude that

{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{1}}$}}={e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}}};{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
.
Since

(we

for the until guard).

\vdots

\mathsf{[VLoop]}

{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {c:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}\ \vdash\ {\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Nmbr}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{2}}$}}}

with
\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{body}$}}
and
\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{until}$}}
passively compatible and
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{t}$}}hen_{2}=({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{body}$}})^{\star};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{until}$}}
. By following the same
approach, it can be shown that both premisses are derivable by taking

\begin{array}[]{lcl}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{body}$}}&=&{e}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{fold}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}}.{e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}}};{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}+{e}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{read}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}}}.{e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{SIZE}$}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{until}$}}&=&{e}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}\ {}}.{e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\ {}};{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\end{array}

for a suitable
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{SIZE}$}}
such that
\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{SIZE}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{SIZE}$}}
.
It is straightforward to check that

\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{body}$}}
and

\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{until}$}}
are passively compatible
because the channels in
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{body}$}}
are different from the ones
appearing in
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{until}$}}
. Moreover,

\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{2}}$}}}\big)=\Game\big({({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{body}$}})^{\star};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{until}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{Nmbr}$}}
.

It remains to show that
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}={{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then}$}}\ {\bowtie}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{else}$}}
is well-defined. We first compute the
normal form of the pseudo types.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathsf{nf}({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then}$}}})&=&{e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}}};\mathsf{nf}({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{2}}$}}})\\
\mathsf{nf}({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{else}$}}})&=&{\neg e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}\ {}};{\neg e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\ {}};{\neg e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}\end{array}

It is immediate to notice that
\mathsf{nf}({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then}$}}})
and
\mathsf{nf}({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{else}$}}})
are mergeable because they are internal choices on
disjoint set of session channels. Therefore,

{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}={{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then}$}}\ {\bowtie}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{else}$}}={e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}}};\mathsf{nf}({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{2}}$}}})\ \oplus\ {\neg e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}\ {}};{\neg e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{bye}}}}\ {}};{\neg e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}

Finally, note that
\Game\big({{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}}\big)={\mathsf{T}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}
.

We now give the main types for the multiparty variant given in (
5.5
) (
cf.
page
5.5
).
Assume
\Delta(u)\equiv{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{POP}$}}^{\prime}
from
Section

3
and consider the
following pseudo-type:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{quit}}}}\ {}.{{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}}}~+~{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{helo}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{AUTH}$}}}

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{AUTH}$}}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{req}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Str}}};{\mathtt{true}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{res}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Bool}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}}

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{MBOX}$}}

\displaystyle\,\triangleq\,{e\land a}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{r}}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{Int}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{NMBR}$}}}~\oplus~{\neg(e\land a)}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}{e}}}}\ {};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}}

such that
\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}_{{\mathsf{{s}}}}}\big)=\mathsf{T}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{{s}}}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{NMBR}$}}
is as
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{then_{2}}$}}
above except that all enabling
conditions are
(e\land a)
, and
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}
is as
{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{EXIT}$}}
except that all enabling conditions are
\neg(e\land a)
.
The typing judgement
{\mathtt{true}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\emptyset}\ \vdash\ {\mathit{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}Init^{\prime}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{s}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{{\mathsf{{s}}}}$}}}
can be obtained
as in the previous case.

8.
Properties of the Type System

In this section we show that a well-typed process (i) behaves as
specified by the global type (
4
) and (ii) is a
WSI of the role played in the global type
(
8
).

8.1.
Conformance

In order to show that any well-typed process adheres to the behaviour
defined by a global type, we relate the semantics of the process with
the one of its specification through a subject reduction result
(
2
).
The operational semantics of specifications is generated by the rules
in
Fig.

12
, where it is implicitly assumed that we work
up-to normal forms, namely the pseudo-types are normalised before and
after transitions.
Notice that the labels are as in (
4.1
) on
page
4.1
but for the fact that they cannot be
conditional actions
e\vdash\alpha
.

\begin{array}[]{lr}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[TReq]}$}\\

Figure 12.
Labelled transitions for specifications

Intuitively, the rules in
Fig.

12
, barred the last three,
state how the specification of a single participant behaves in a
session
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
and are instrumental to establish subject
reduction.

Rules
\mathsf{[TReq]}
and
\mathsf{[TAcc]}
account for a specification that
initiates a new session by projecting (on
{\mathsf{{0}}}
and
{\mathsf{{p}}}
,
resp.) the global type associated with the shared name
{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}
in

\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
can use arbitrary guards in the projections

\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})\!\!\upharpoonright\!{\mathsf{{p}}}
as long as the normal form of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}

matches the one of the projection.
Rule
\mathsf{[TRcv]}
accounts for the reception of a message.
Dually, rule
\mathsf{[TSend]}
accounts for an endpoint that performs one
of its outputs.
Rule
\mathsf{[TSeq]}
relies on the definition of sequential composition
of specifications (
cf.
page
7.2
); observe that the
case in which all pseudo-types in
\Delta_{1}
are of the form
{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
is precluded because we work up-to normal form of pseudo-types.
Finally, an iterative local type can be skipped (rule

\mathsf{[TLoop_{0}]}
), executed once (rule
\mathsf{[TLoop_{1}]}
), or be
unfolded (rule
\mathsf{[TLoop_{2}]}
).
The last three rules in
Fig.

12
state how specifications
of systems behave.
Rule
\mathsf{[TInit]}
initiates a new session
\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}
by assigning
each participant with a type that matches the corresponding projection
of the global type.
Rules
\mathsf{[TCom_{1}]}
and
\mathsf{[TCom_{2}]}
establish how specifications
send and receive messages through queues.

\begin{array}[]{c@{\hspace{1cm}}c@{\hspace{1cm}}c}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[CEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\&}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ltimes{{{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}}}}\end{array}\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[CSeq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}}\qquad{}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ltimes{{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}}}}\end{array}\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[CLoop]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star}}}\end{array}\\[20.00003pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[CSend]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\exists i\in I\;\colon\;e_{i}\&\wedge{}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ltimes{{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}}}\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[CRcv]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\forall i\in I\;\colon\;e_{i}\&\wedge{}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ltimes{{\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}}}\end{array}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

Figure 13.
Consistency relation
between stores and pseudo-types

The behaviour of processes depends on the stores they run
on. Consequently, we compare process and specifications with respect
to stores; concretely, we only consider the behaviour of processes
running on stores that are consistent with the guards in the
pseudo-types of the specifications.
The consistency predicate
{\_}\ltimes{\_}
relates stores with
pseudo-types and is inductively defined by the rules
in
Fig.

13
.
Intuitively,
{}\ltimes{}
holds if
\sigma
does not falsify any guard
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
; which is checked by rules
\mathsf{[CEnd]}
,
\mathsf{[CSend]}

and
\mathsf{[CRcv]}
, where
\&
means
{e}\downarrow\sigma=\mathtt{true}

or
{e}\downarrow\sigma
undefined.

The notion of consistency is then extended to type judgments as follows.

Definition 1

(Consistency)
.

A store
\sigma
is
consistent
with a judgement
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
,
written
\ltimes{(;;;)}
, if

(1)

\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{Y}$}})}\cup\mathrm{dom}{(\Gamma)}\subseteq\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}

(2)

\forall x\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Gamma)}\;\colon\;{}\ {}\ {\sigma(x)}{}{\Gamma(x)}

(3)

{e}\downarrow\sigma=\mathtt{true}

(4)

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}\;\colon\;{}\ltimes{\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}}
.

A store
\sigma
is consistent with a type judgement

{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
if
\sigma
contains an assignment for any free
name of
S
(
Item

1
); the values assigned to variables should
match the type assigned by the environment
\Gamma

(
Item

2
). Besides, the typing assumption
e
and the guards
in the pseudo-types in
\Delta
should hold when evaluated over

\sigma
(
Item

3
and
Item

4
).

Theorem 2

(Subject reduction)
.

If
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
,
\ltimes{(;;;)}
, and
\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle
then there exist

\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Delta^{\prime}
such that

(1)

if
\alpha=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}
then
\Delta\xrightarrow{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\Delta^{\prime}
for a sort
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}
; moreover, if

{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}
then there is
x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{X}$}
such that

{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\ \vdash\ {S^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}},\qquad\sigma^{\prime}(x)=\mathsf{v},\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}};S^{\prime};\Delta^{\prime})}

(2)

if
\alpha=\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}
then
\Delta\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\Delta^{\prime}
with
{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}
,
{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {S^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}}
, and

\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime};S^{\prime};\Delta^{\prime})}

(3)

otherwise
\Delta\xrightarrow{\alpha}\Delta^{\prime}
, and
{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {S^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}}
,

\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime};S^{\prime};\Delta^{\prime})}
.

The typing rules in
Section

7
ensure the semantic conformance
of processes with the behaviour prescribed by their types.
Here, we define conformance in terms of
conditional that

simulation relates states and specifications.
Our definition is standard, except for input actions, for which
specifications have to simulate only inputs of messages with the
expected type (i.e., systems are not responsible when receiving
ill-typed messages).

Define
\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\Longrightarrow}}\,=\xrightarrow{\tau}^{*}\xrightarrow{\alpha}
.
Let
\Delta\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}\,
mean that there are
\Delta^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{v}

such that
\Delta\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}\,\Delta^{\prime}
.

Definition 3

(Conditional simulation)
.

A relation
\mathbb{R}
between pairs state-specification is a

conditional simulation
if for any

(\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle,\Delta)\in\mathbb{R}
, if

\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle

then

(1)

if
\alpha=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}
then
there exists
\Delta^{\prime}
such that

\Delta\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}\,\Delta^{\prime}
and if

{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}
then there exists
x
such that

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[{x}\mapsto{\mathsf{v}}]
and

(\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle,\Delta^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}

(2)

otherwise,
\Delta\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\Longrightarrow}}\,\Delta^{\prime}
and

(\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle,\Delta^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}
.

We write
\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle\precsim\Delta
if there is a
conditional simulation
\mathbb{R}
such that

(\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle,\Delta)\in\mathbb{R}
.

By (
1
), only inputs of
S
with the expected type have to
be matched by
\Delta
(recall rule
\mathsf{[TRec]}
in

Fig.

12
), while it is no longer expected to conform to the
specification after an ill-typed input (i.e., not allowed by

\Delta
).

Conformance follows by straightforward coinduction from
2
.

Theorem 4

(Conformance)
.

If
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
and
\ltimes{(;;;)}
then
\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle\precsim\Delta
.

Proof 8.1
.

Using
2
it is straightforward to show that

\mathbb{R}=\{(\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle,\Delta)\;\big|\;{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}\text{ and }\ltimes{(;;;)}\}

is a conditional simulation.

8.2.
WSI by Typing

We show that well-typed processes are WSIs (
6.8
on
page
6.8
).
First, we relate the runs of a global type with those of its
corresponding specifications.
Then, we state the correspondence between the runs of specifications
and well-typed implementations.
A set of implementations covering a global type
\mathcal{G}
can exhibit more
behaviour than the runs of
\mathcal{G}
.
Nonetheless, we use WSI with our subject reduction property
(
cf.

3
and
) to
characterise valid implementations.

Given a specification
\Delta
such that
\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
is in
normal form for all
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}

generated by
\Delta

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTCom_{1}]}$}\\

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTIt_{1}]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta^{\star})}\end{array}\hfill\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTIt_{2}]}$}\\

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTEnd]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\Delta\ \text{$\mathsf{end}$-only}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\epsilon\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta,\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]})}\end{array}\hskip 28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTSeq]}$}\\
\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta_{1})\qquad r_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta_{2})}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}r_{1}r_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta_{1};\Delta_{2})}\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 14.
Runs of specifications

Rule
\mathsf{[RTCom_{1}]}
accounts for runs starting with output actions

\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{j}}\rangle
performed by an endpoint

{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
and followed by a run
r
of the continuation.
Rule
\mathsf{[RTCom_{2}]}
analogously deals with inputs.
Rules
\mathsf{[RTIt_{1}]}
and
\mathsf{[RTIt_{2}]}
unfold an iterative type;
one iteration is mandatory while the additional ones are optional.
The remaining rules are self-explanatory.
Since all types in
\Delta
are in normal form (and therefore
inconsistent guards may appear only in sub terms
{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
), the
rules in
Fig.

14
do not generate runs with
actions that cannot be fired.
The following two results establish the correspondence between the
denotational and operational semantics of specifications.

Lemma 5
.

Let
\Delta
be a specification such that for all
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
,
\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}
is in normal form. If

\Delta\xrightarrow{\tau}\Delta^{\prime}
, then for all

either:

•

r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)
, or

•

\langle{{\mathsf{{p}}}},{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\rangle r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)
, or else

•

\langle{{\mathsf{{q}}}},{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}}\rangle r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)
.

.

,

.

7
below ensures that

ensures that a local specification can be
