Theorem 7

(Coverage & projections)
.

Let
{}({})\,\triangleq\,{}
be a global type with
\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{G})=\{{\mathsf{{p_{0}}}},\ldots,{\mathsf{{p_{n}}}}\}
and
\Delta={\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{0}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{0},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{n}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{n},\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}
such that
\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}\big)=\mathsf{nf}({\mathsf{G}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{i}}})
for
all
0\leq i\leq n
.
Then
\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})\ \Subset\ \mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)
.

Theorem 8

(Typeability & coverage)
.

Let
{}({})\,\triangleq\,{}
be a global type with
\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{G})=\{{\mathsf{{p_{0}}}},\ldots,{\mathsf{{p_{n}}}}\}
and
\Delta={\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{0}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{0},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{n}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{n},\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}
such that
\Game\big({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}\big)=\mathsf{nf}({\mathsf{G}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{i}}})
for
all
0\leq i\leq n
.
If
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{i}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}
then the set
\mathbb{I}=\{{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}\ |\ {e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {{{}{}_{{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{}}}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\ \wedge\ \iota({\mathsf{{p}}}_{i})=P\}
covers
\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)
, i.e.,

\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)\Subset\mathcal{R}\mathbb{I}
.

The result above relies on an auxiliary result that shows that each
run
r
of an specification
\Delta
can be covered by a well-typed
implementation
@
of
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
in which
P
plays role

{\mathsf{{p}}}
(details are provided in
,

). Since
\Subset
is transitive, we
conclude that any well-typed process is a WSI of a role in a
choreography.

Corollary 9

(WSI of well-typed processes)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
be a global type,
{\mathsf{{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{G})
. If
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
,
P
is a whole-spectrum implementation of
{\mathsf{{p}}}
.

9.
Conclusion and Related Work

WSI rejects implementations of a role that persistently avoids the
execution of some branches in a choreography.
Although WSI is defined as a relation between the traces of a global
type and those of its candidate implementations, it can be checked by
using multiparty session types.
As standard, the soundness of our type system –guaranteed by the
conformance of the typing (
4
)– ensures that the
behaviour of well-typed implementations follows the protocol described
by the global type (i.e., global types are interpreted as
constraints).
Moreover, we show that
(i)
the sets of the projections of a global
type
\mathcal{G}
preserves all the traces in
\mathcal{G}

(
7
); and
(ii)
a well-typed
process can be used to obtain any trace of the projections of a global
type when interacting in a proper context
(
8
).
These two results and the fact that the covering relation is
transitive allow us to conclude that any well-typed process is a WSI
of a role in a choreography (
9
), i.e.,
global types are interpreted as obligations.

9.1.
Behavioural types

In this paper we have followed the rich line of research fostering the
application of behavioural types to concurrent programs.
Examples of similar approaches are those to guarantee properties of
complex concurrent systems such as in the seminal work of Kobayashi on
deadlock freedom for the
\pi
-calculus
[
29
,
32
]
or
progress analysis for choreographies
[
16
]
, information flow
analysis
[
31
,
8
]
, design-by-contract for message-passing
systems
[
3
]
, or self-adaptation
[
15
,
12
]
. Our type
system is more restrictive
than
[
26
,
1
,
5
,
11
,
7
]

as it rules out sound but not exhaustive implementations, which
previous type systems considered well-typed.
To the best of our knowledge, the only proposal dealing with complete
(i.e., exhaustive) realisations in a behavioural context
is
[
10
]
but this approach focuses
on non-deterministic implementation languages.
WSI coincides with projection
realisability
[
33
,
42
,
10
]

when implementation languages feature non-deterministic internal
choices.
On the contrary, WSI provides a finer criterion to distinguish
deterministic implementations, as illustrated by the motivating
example in the introduction.

illustrates that WSI can be recast into
other computational models or settings, e.g., in the context of
guarded automata. In such context, WSI admits a more succinct
characterisation, and hence, would appear as a more amenable
definition for WSI (when compared against the definition given in
Section 6). However, we remark that several technicalities in our
proposal arise when developing a static and modular technique for
ensuring WSI. MSTs are perhaps the most widely accepted technique for
developing static and modular nal verification techniques for
multiparty interactions, and to link their formal specification to
programming languages, even at the expenses of dense technical
definition (e.g., three different languages, static and run-time
semantics, well-formed conditions, etc).
It may be the case that similar techniques could be developed for
ensuring WSI implementations in other contexts, e.g., guarded
automata, but the starting point is not that obvious. It could be the
case that some model-checking solution could be developed but the
existence of a feasible and simpler approach is an interesting
question that requires future work (note that the definitions in

are based on an existential quantification
over possible contexts, which could be problematic for obtaining an
effective procedure).

9.2.
WSI and subtyping

The standard subtyping relation
[
22
]
is not suitable for WSI
because the liberal elimination of internal choices prevents WSI. For
example, standard subtyping for output prefixes allows process
{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}(v).\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}}\,{e}
to have local type

{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}};(\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}+\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{2}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}})
,
and this clearly violates WSI. The problematic aspect is that
subtyping allows for a liberal implementation of internal choices,
whereas WSI requires a precise implementation of all branches in a
choice. This means that a WSI of a type
\mathsf{T}
, may not be a WSI of a
subtype of
\mathsf{T}
.
The investigation of suitable forms of subtyping for WSIs is scope for
future work.
To some extent our proposal is related to the fair subtyping approach
in
[
41
]
, where refinement is
studied under the fairness assumption: fair subtyping differs from
usual subtyping when considering infinite computations but WSI differs
from partial implementation also when considering finite computations.

9.3.
Languages for types and processes

Our
notation for session types combines interaction and branching in one
single operation. This choice was made for the sake of conciseness.
The combination of interaction and branching in one single operation
has been used in several works, albeit in different flavours
(e.g.,
[
13
]
,
[
34
]
,
[
2
]

and
[
19
]
).
In
[
34
]
the notation is more flexible than ours, allowing
(syntactically) global types of the form

{\mathsf{{p}}}\rightarrowtriangle{\mathsf{{q}}}\colon\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d_{1}}};\mathsf{G}_{1}+{\mathsf{{r}}}\rightarrowtriangle{\mathsf{{s}}}\colon\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{2}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d_{2}}};\mathsf{G}_{2}

(9.1)

which our syntax does not allows (as it requires
{\mathsf{{p}}}={\mathsf{{r}}}
). However, the work in
[
34
]
rules out types as the one in
(
9.1
) when
{\mathsf{{p}}}\not={\mathsf{{r}}}
by the well-formedness rules
(i.e., knowledge of choice).
Our notation simplifies well-formedness by having this requirement in
the syntax. Another line of
work
[
13
,
2
]
uses a
syntax more restrictive than ours: in types of the form (
9.1
),
[
13
,
2
]
require

{\mathsf{{p}}}={\mathsf{{r}}}
and
{\mathsf{{q}}}={\mathsf{{s}}}
. Such restriction prevents types
of the form

{\mathsf{{p}}}\rightarrowtriangle{\mathsf{{q}}}\colon\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d_{1}}};{\mathsf{{r}}}\rightarrowtriangle{\mathsf{{s}}}\colon\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{2}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d_{2}}};\mathsf{end}+{\mathsf{{r}}}\rightarrowtriangle{\mathsf{{s}}}\colon\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{2}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d_{2}}};{\mathsf{{p}}}\rightarrowtriangle{\mathsf{{q}}}\colon\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{1}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d_{1}}};\mathsf{end}

which are instead well-formed in our theory. The notation introduced
in this paper differs from usual syntaxes for session types in two
other significant ways, which we highlight below. Firstly, we use
iteration instead of recursion. In order to verify WSI, our proof
system needs to statically determine that the body of each iteration:
(1) happens at least once (if interactions in the body are not
executed the implementation is not WSI), and (2) terminates (to ensure
that interactions in the continuation of an iteration will be
executed). For this reason, we need
finitary interactions
in
the language for processes. However, usual interpretation of recursive
types (where maximal fixpoints are assumed) is not finitary.
Therefore, the use of standard recursive session types would introduce
a mismatch between the semantics of programs (where we need finitary
iterations) with the usual interpretation of recursive types (where
maximal fixpoints are assumed).

Therefore, the static verification of WSI requires a form of recursion
more restrictive than the one in previous work on session
types
[
26
,
1
]
, where the number of iterations
is limited. This restriction is on the lines
of
[
10
]
that also considers finite
traces, and is close to for-loops of programming languages. The
extension of our theory with a more general form of iteration is scope
for future work.

Because of the chosen notation for interaction-choice, iteration
requires some care to ensure that all participants agree on whether
another iteration should be executed. We do this by requiring that
each iteration has exactly one controller-participant as
in
[
10
]
.

Secondly, we use sequential composition to allow clear delimitation of
structured constructs (loops and conditional statements) hence ease
static verification and its modularity.

We disregarded delegation in our framework because its addition would
greatly increase the complexity of our framework. A type system
guaranteeing WSI under delegation in our context should not be
problematic to device at the cost of a higher technical complexity.

9.4.
Choreography languages

In the literature, the term ‘choreography’ is used in two different ways.
The first one, which we follow, considers choreographies as abstract
specifications
[
30
]
or formal models (e.g., Petri
Nets
[
37
]
or session
types
[
26
,
1
]
) where choices are
non-deterministic, in the sense that branches are not associated to
conditions (e.g., as in if-then-else statements). The W3C’s Web
Services Choreography Description Language (WS-CDL)
[
30
]
is
an XML-based specification language where choices are enumerated
without expressing conditions. In the context of multiparty session
types
[
26
,
1
]
, choreographies are modelled as
global types which, by projection, produce abstractions of processes
(with non-deterministic choices in the sense specified above) that can
be used for static and dynamic verification. On this thread, we also
mention works that study partial vs complete
realisations
[
37
,
10
]
.Remarkably, WSI coincides with projection
realisability
[
33
,
42
,
10
]

when the language adopted to implement choreographies features
non-deterministic internal choices.
On the contrary, WSI provides a finer criterion to distinguish
deterministic implementations, as illustrated by the motivating
example in the introduction. The second usage is in the context of
choreographic programming (e.g.,
[
9
]
,
[
36
]
,
[
18
]
)
where a choreography is a built in construct for programs (hence
deterministic) used to produce correct-by-design code. In such
contexts there is no need to resolve non-deterministic choices since
the language is deterministic.

9.5.
POP 2 specification

We captured (to the best of our understanding) the most salient
aspects of the communication of the POP2 protocol according to its
official informal specification in the state-machines in pages 16–18
of RFC937
[
6
]
. We have focussed on the interaction structure
and left out non-functional aspects such as timeouts that are beyond
the scope of this paper. We have adopted a simplification on the
interaction structure for the sake of a simpler presentation: POP2 has
a branch ‘quit’ from state ‘SIZE’ (see
[
6
]
, page 16) whereas
our model
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{SIZE}$}}
in
Section

5
does not have a
‘quit’ option. To quit the protocol from state
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{SIZE}$}}
one
needs to go back to state
{\mathsf{G}}_{\resizebox{}{196608}{$\mathtt{FOLD}$}}
(which offers the option
‘quit’ as also the corresponding state in the RFC). In our formalism,
the end of a loop has to be signalled by exactly one message (in our
case message ‘fold’). We could have encoded this extra ‘quit’ option
from state ‘SIZE’ by either:

•

extending our choreography language to allow a set of possible
messages to signal termination of a loop, which would not add
considerable challenge but would increase the technicalities in the
presentation;

•

encoding the exact POP2 pattern by introducing intermediary
states with additional messages which would have made the
presentation of the protocol itself less clear.

Our goal was to demonstrate that our typing language can model
realistic communications, not to provide an analysis of POP2 on its
own sake (which we leave as future work), hence our simplification of
the protocol.

Appendix A
Auxiliary Properties of Typing

In this section we provide technical details and auxiliary properties
of the type system, which are used in the proof of the main results of
the paper.

A.1.
Normal form
\mathsf{nf}({\_,\_})

Below we state useful results about the normal form of
pseudo-types. We start by introducing a well-founded relation on
pseudo-types, which will be used for inductive proofs. The relation

<
on pseudo-types is defined in terms of the following function

\omega:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}\to{\rm Nature}
:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}\omega({e}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}})&1\\
\omega({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}})&1+\max\ {\{\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i})\}}_{i\in I}\\
\omega({\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}})&1+\max\ {\{\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i})\}}_{i\in I}\\
\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2})&2*\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1})+\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2})\\
\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star})&1+\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})\end{array}

We say
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}<{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
iff
\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1})<\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2})
. It is
straightforward to check that
\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})>0
for all

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
. Consequently,
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}},<)
is well-founded.

Lemma 1
.

\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
is defined for any
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
(i.e., it terminates).

Proof A.1
.

The proof follows by showing that the function

f(e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})=\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})
is a variant function for the definition
of
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
in
Fig.

8
. We proceed
by case analysis on the equations in
Fig.

8

(we illustrate the interesting cases below).

(2)

f(e,{{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}})=1+\max\ {\{\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i})\}}_{i\in I}>\omega({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i})=f(e_{i}\wedge e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i})

for all
i\in I\setminus J
.

\begin{array}[]{lcl}f(e,{\big({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\big);{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&=&\omega(\big({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}\big);{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}})\\

Lemma 2
.

For all
e,e^{\prime}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
, if
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})={e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
,
then
\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})={e^{\prime\prime}\wedge e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
.

Proof A.2
.

By well-founded induction on
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}},<)
. The proof follows by case
analysis on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
.

Lemma 3
.

For all
e,e^{\prime}

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\

Otherwise (

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})^{\star};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})^{\star};\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})^{\star};\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})^{\star};\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})&\textit{by ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})^{\star};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}
: Then,

\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})})=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}})})

If
\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}})={e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
, the proof
follows by
2
, otherwise:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}})^{\star}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}})})^{\star}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(13)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}})^{\star}&{\it by\ ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(13)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

Lemma 4
.

If
e\iff\mathtt{false}
then
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})={e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
and
e^{\prime}\iff\mathtt{false}
.

Proof A.4
.

By well-founded induction on
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}},<)
. The proof follows by case
analysis on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
.

Lemma 5
.

For all
e
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
,

\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})
.

Proof A.5
.

By well-founded induction on
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}},<)
. The proof follows by case
analysis on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(1)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\wedge e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:nf-aux}}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(1)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}});\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
\end{array}

The case in which
J=\{i\in I\;\big|\;(e\land e_{i}^{\prime})\iff\mathtt{false}\}=I
follows immediately. Otherwise, we proceed as
follows

\begin{array}[]{rll}&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\wedge e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})}}}}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(2)}\\
&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i},\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})})}}}}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:nf-aux}}\\
&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i},\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})})}}}}&\textit{by ind.\ hyp.}\\
&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\wedge e,{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}}})}}}}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:nf-aux}}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(2)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}});{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
: It
follows analogously to the previous one.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2});\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}))})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(7)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})})&\textit{by ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})})&\textit{by\ ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})})&\textit{by\ ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2});{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(7)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{1}
:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{1};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

If
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})={e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
, then the proof follows
straightforwardly
from

8
(8). Otherwise,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rll}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:nf-aux}}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\

Lemma 6
.

If
e\implies e^{\prime}
, then
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
.

Proof A.6
.

By well-founded induction on
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}},<)
. The proof follows by case
analysis on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime\prime},{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&={e\land e^{\prime\prime}\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(1)}\\
&={e\land e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}&e\implies e^{\prime}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}});{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}})}}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(7)}\end{array}

If
I=J
, then the case follows immediately. Otherwise,

\begin{array}[]{rll}&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\backslash J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\wedge e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})}}}}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(4)}\\
&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\backslash J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\wedge e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}})}}}}&\textit{by ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(4)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
: It
follows analogously to the previous one.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2});{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:nf-seq-composition}}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})})&\textit{by ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}
: Then,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star};{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},\mathsf{end}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,\mathsf{end}})&e\implies e^{\prime}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star};\mathsf{end}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};\mathsf{end}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\end{array}

Lemma 7
.

Equality
\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})
holds for all
e
and
e^{\prime}
such that
\neg(e\iff\mathtt{false})
and
e\implies e^{\prime}
.

Proof A.7
.

By well-founded induction on
({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}},<)
. The proof follows by case
analysis on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime\prime},{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime\prime}\land e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}});&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}});&e\land e^{\prime\prime}\land e^{\prime}\iff e\land e^{\prime\prime}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}});({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}))}}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(7)}\end{array}

If
I=J
, then the case follows immediately. Otherwise,

\begin{array}[]{rll}&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\backslash J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\wedge e,({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}))})}}}}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(4)}\\
&={{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I\backslash J}{e_{i}\wedge e}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.\mathsf{nf}({e_{i}\wedge e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})}}}}&{\it by\ ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})}}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(4)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}});{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(7)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{e_{i}}\Yleft{{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}}
: It
follows analogously to the previous one.

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2}
: Then,

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2});({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:nf-seq-composition}}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&{\it by\ ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:nf-seq-composition}}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,({\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{2});{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(9)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}
: Then,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star};({e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime})})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,{e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star}});\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&e\implies e^{\prime}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{1}^{\star};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(11)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\end{array}

Lemma 8
.

\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})=\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}){\bowtie}\mathsf{nf}({e,{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})
.

Proof A.8
.

By straightforward induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}{\bowtie}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
.

A.2.
Typing

We write
\mathrm{var}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
to denote the variables appearing in the
expressions occurring on the pseudo-type
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
. It is
straightforwardly extended to specifications
\mathrm{var}({\Delta})
.

Lemma 9
.

If
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
, then
\mathrm{var}({\Delta})\subseteq\mathrm{var}({e})\cup\mathtt{fx}(S)\cup\mathtt{bx}(S)
.

Proof A.9
.

By straightforward structural induction on the typing judgment.

Lemma 10
.

If
{}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {}
then
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma,x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
.

Proof A.10
.

By induction on the structure of the proof
{}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {}
.

Lemma 11
.

If
{}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {}
then

•

\mathtt{fx}(P)\cup\mathtt{fy}(P)\cup\mathtt{fx}(e)\subseteq\Gamma

•

\forall\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\mathtt{fy}(P)\;\colon\;\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
.

Proof A.11
.

By induction of the derivation of
{}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {}
and inspecting the rules
in
Fig.

10
and
11
.

Lemma 12
.

If
{}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {}
and
P\equiv Q
, then
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {Q}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}}
and

\mathsf{nf}({\Delta})=\mathsf{nf}({\Delta^{\prime}})
.

Proof A.12
.

By induction on the proof of
P\equiv Q
.

•

P;(Q;R)\equiv(P;Q);R
follows by inductive hypothesis and
associativity of
;
over pseudo types (
Fig.

8
(9)).

•

;P\equiv P;\equiv P
follows by using

7
and

6
.

Lemma 13
.

If
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}

\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})={\mathtt{false}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}

Proof A.13
.

Follows by induction on the structure of
P
. The interesting cases
are

•

\mathsf{[VSeq]}
: Then,
P=P_{1};P_{2}
,
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{1}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}

and

and
\Delta_{1};\Delta_{2}
defined. By
inductive hypothesis, if

then

\mathsf{nf}({\Delta_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}})={\mathtt{false}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
for

i=1,2
. Then, the result follows by the definition of
;
and rule (4) in
Fig.

8
.

•

\mathsf{[VIf]}
: Then,
P=~{e^{\prime}}~~P_{1}~~{P_{2}}
,

{e\wedge e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{1}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}
, and

{e\wedge\neg e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}
and

\Delta_{1}{\bowtie}\Delta_{2}
. By inductive hypothesis, if
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{i})}
then
\mathsf{nf}({\Delta_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}})={\mathtt{false}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}
for
i=1,2
. Then, the results follows by
definition of
{\bowtie}
.

Lemma 14
.

If
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}
, then for all
e^{\prime}
there is
\Delta^{\prime}
such

•

{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}}

•

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}
,
\Delta^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}={e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}

Proof A.14
.

Let
\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
and
\Delta^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}={\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}
. We show that
\mathsf{nf}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})=\mathsf{nf}({{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
by induction on the structure of the proof for
the judgment
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}
. We first
assume that
\neg(e\land e^{\prime}\iff\mathtt{false})
, and show

•

\mathsf{[VReq]}
,
\mathsf{[VAcc]}
: Follow by inductive hypothesis.

•

\mathsf{[VRcv]}
: Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}
, and

\forall i\in I\;\colon\;{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma,x_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}\ \vdash\ {P_{i}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}
.
Similarly,

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}}

and

\forall i\in I\;\colon\;{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma,x_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}}\ \vdash\ {P_{i}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}}}

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({\mathtt{true},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{i}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}}&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}$}\\
&=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}^{\prime}})}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(5)}\\
&=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}})}&{\it by\ ind.\ hyp.}\\
&=\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}})}&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},\displaystyle{\sum_{i\in I}{{e}\Yleft{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}_{i}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{\mbox{\scriptsize$i$}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{i}}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(5)}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({e\land e^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}$}\\
&=\mathsf{nf}({\mathtt{true},{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}};{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})&\textit{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:normalization-pseudo}(6)}\end{array}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}={e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}};{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}

\begin{array}[]{rll}\mathsf{nf}({\mathtt{true},{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}})&=\mathsf{nf}({\mathtt{true},{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}};{e\land e^{\prime}}\Yleft{\mathsf{end}}})&\textit{by def.\ of ${\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}^{\prime}$}\\

A.3.
Consistency

Lemma 16
.

If
{}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}
, then
{\sigma^{\prime}}\ltimes{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}}
for any
\sigma^{\prime}

such that
\sigma(x)=\sigma^{\prime}(x)
for all
x\in\mathrm{var}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
.

Proof A.16
.

By structural induction on the structure of
{}\ltimes{}
and by
noticing that
{e}\downarrow{(\sigma\cap\sigma^{\prime})}={e}\downarrow\sigma

for every expression
e
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}
.

Lemma 17
.

If
x\not\in\mathrm{var}({{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}})
and
{}\ltimes{}
then

Proof A.17

It follows

\mathsf{[VRcv]}

Lemma 21
.

Let
{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}

(1)

(2)

\Delta^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}=\Delta^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}

{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta)}

\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(\Delta^{\prime\prime};e\land E\land e^{\prime};P^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime})}

\mathsf{[VReq]}
Then,

\mathsf{[SReq]}

•

\mathsf{[TRcv]}

Then,

There are two cases,

(1)

\alpha=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{j}
with
j\in I
. Then,

\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathbb{T}}_{j}
.
The proof is completed by taking
P^{\prime}=P_{j}
, and
\widetilde{\beta}=\epsilon
, and
E=e^{\prime}=\mathtt{true}
, and

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma[{x_{j}}\mapsto{\mathsf{v}}]
, and

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma,x_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}_{i}
, and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\Delta^{\prime}
. Note that
\langle{P},{\sigma}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle

by
\mathsf{[SRec]}
. Condition
\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(\Delta^{\prime\prime};e;P^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime})}
follows straightforwardly
from
\sigma\ltimes{(\Delta;e;P;\Gamma)}
.

(2)

\alpha\neq\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u}}^{{\mathsf{{n}}}}(\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}})
. Hence,
\mathtt{n}(\alpha)\cap\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}=\emptyset
. The case follows analogously
to rule
\mathsf{[VReq]}
.

Lemma 22
.

If
\langle{P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle
, then

{e}\downarrow\sigma=\mathtt{true}
.

Proof A.22
.

By straightforward induction on the structure of the proof

\langle{P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle
.

A.4.
Subject reduction

We first state an auxiliary property about the semantics of systems.

Lemma 23
.

If
\langle{S},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle
then

\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}\subseteq\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma^{\prime})}
.

Proof A.23
.

By induction on the derivation of
\langle{S},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle
inspecting the rules in

Fig.

4
and
5
.

See
2

Proof A.24
.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of
\langle{S},{\sigma}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle
which may end with
the application of one of the rules in
Fig.

(on
page

) or in
Fig.

5
(on
page
5
).
It is a simple observation that if
\alpha\neq\tau
then

must be a process, so the proof ends with one of the rules in

Fig.

4
.

$$\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{M},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{P},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{\underbrace{~N~~M}_{=S}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{P},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\end{array}\qquad\text{with }S^{\prime}=P$$and the only applicable rule to type $S$ is \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VLoop]}$}:
$$\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {N}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {M}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}\qquad\Delta_{1}\mbox{ and }\Delta_{2}\mbox{ passively compatible}\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~N~~M}\ \triangleright\ {{\Delta_{1}^{\star}};\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}$$with $\Delta=\Delta_{1}^{\star};\Delta_{2}$.
The inductive hypothesis requires to have $\sigma\ltimes{(;;M;\Delta_{2})}$,
which easily follows because~\eqref{cons2:eq}-\eqref{cons4:eq} are
immediate from $\ltimes{(;;;)}$ and~\eqref{cons5:eq} follows from
\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:const-pseudo-typing-and-store-correspondence}.
Note that $\alpha$ has to be an input label
$\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}$ because $M$ is a choice process.
Hence, there exist $\Gamma^{\prime}$ and
$\Delta_{2}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\Delta_{2}^{\prime}$ and if
${}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}$ then
$${e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}^{\prime}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}};P;\Delta_{2}^{\prime})}$$Then, take $\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{2}^{\prime}$ and note that by \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[TLoop_{0}]}$}
$\Delta=\Delta_{1}^{\star};\Delta_{2}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\Delta^{\prime}$.

\@@amsgather
and, as in the case \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SLoopEnd]}$},
the only applicable rule to type $S$ is \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VLoop]}$}:
$$\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {N}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}}\qquad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {M}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}\qquad\Delta_{1}\mbox{ and }\Delta_{2}\mbox{ passively compatible}\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {~N~~M}\ \triangleright\ {{\Delta_{1}^{\star}};\Delta_{2}}}\end{array}$$with $\Delta=\Delta_{1}^{\star};\Delta_{2}$.
The inductive hypothesis requires to show
$\sigma\ltimes{(;;N;\Delta_{1})}$, which holds because~\eqref{cons2:eq}-\eqref{cons4:eq} are immediate from $\ltimes{(;;;)}$ and~\eqref{cons5:eq} follows from

Note that $\alpha$ has to be an input label
$\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}$ because $N$ is a choice process.
Hence, there exist $\Gamma^{\prime}$ and

${}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}$ then
$${e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}^{\prime}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}};P;\Delta_{1}^{\prime})}$$Then, take $\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{1}^{\prime};\Delta_{1}^{\star}$ and note that by

\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@{\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SCom_{1}]}$}} In this case:
$$\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{P},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}}\langle{P^{\prime}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{\underbrace{P{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}_{=S}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\tau}\langle{\underbrace{P^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}\cdot\mathsf{v}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}_{=S^{\prime}}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}$$and, by rule \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VPar]}$}, we have the following typing for $S$:
$$\qquad\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {P}\ \triangleright\ {\overbrace{\Delta\setminus\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:\vec{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\}}^{=\Delta_{1}}}\quad{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}\ \triangleright\ {\overbrace{\Delta|_{\mbox{$\mathbb{U}$}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:\vec{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}}^{=\Delta_{2}}}\quad\Delta_{1}\text{ and }\Delta_{2}\text{ independent}\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}$$where the typing of the queue ${\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}$ is obtained by

Note that $\Delta=\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}$.

$$\qquad\Delta_{1}\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\quad\text{with}\quad{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\quad{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {P^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}_{1}}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime};P^{\prime};\Delta^{\prime}_{1})}$$for some environment $\Gamma^{\prime}$.
We now show that we can type $S^{\prime}$ with

$$\qquad\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\begin{array}[]{c}{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {P^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}^{\prime}}\qquad\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\qquad{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}\vec{\mathsf{v}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}\ \triangleright\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}\vec{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.2,0.3}\vec{\mathsf{v}}}\cdot\mathsf{v}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}}\ \triangleright\ {\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}:\vec{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}}\ \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VQueue]}$}\end{array}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {S^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}}}\ \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VPar]}$}$$where the judgement in the premiss of \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VQueue]}$} holds by

\par We now show that we can type $S^{\prime}$ with

\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@{\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SPar]}$}} In this case we have:

\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@[\eqref{sr:in}]if $\alpha=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}\mathsf{v}$ then

${e_{1}\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\ \vdash\ {S_{1}^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}^{\prime}}$ and
$\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e_{1}\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime},x:{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}};S_{1}^{\prime};\Delta_{1}^{\prime})}$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[\eqref{sr:out}]if $\alpha=\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\mathsf{v}$ then
$\Delta_{1}\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}$ with
${}\ {}\ {\mathsf{v}}{}{{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.543,0.4883}\mathsf{d}}}$,
$\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e_{1}\land e^{\prime};;S_{1}^{\prime};\Delta_{1}^{\prime})}$, and
${e_{1}\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {}\ \vdash\ {S_{1}^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}^{\prime}}$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@[\eqref{sr:other}]otherwise
$\Delta_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}$,
$\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e_{1}\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime};S_{1}^{\prime};\Delta_{1}^{\prime})}$, and
${e_{1}\land e^{\prime}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {S_{1}^{\prime}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{1}^{\prime}}$
\end{enumerate}In the first two cases $\Delta_{1}^{\prime}$ and $\Delta_{2}$ are independent
because $\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1}^{\prime})}=\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1})}$ and $\Delta_{1}$ and
$\Delta_{2}$ are independent.
In the latter case, we observe that
$\sigma^{\prime}\ltimes{(e_{1}\land e^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime};S_{1}^{\prime};\Delta_{1}^{\prime})}$ implies
$\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{1}^{\prime})}\subseteq\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma^{\prime})}$; moreover, $\sigma^{\prime}$ can
contain a new session, say $\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}$ only if the rule
\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SInit]}$} had been used to derive the transition.
In this case, the side condition of \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SInit]}$} guarantees that
$\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\not\in\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)}$ and therefore they do not occur in
$S_{2}$ and therefore they are not in $\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta_{2})}$ since
$\ltimes{(;;;)}$.
The judgement ${e_{2}}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma^{\prime}}\ \vdash\ {S_{2}}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta_{2}}$ holds
because condition
$\mathtt{fx}(S_{2})\cap(\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma^{\prime})}\setminus\mathrm{dom}{(\sigma)})=\emptyset$
makes $\mathtt{fx}(S_{2})\cap\mathrm{dom}{(\Gamma^{\prime})}=\emptyset$, hence we apply
\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:weakening-env-in-runs}.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@{\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SNew]}$}} In this case we have:
$$\qquad\qquad\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{S_{1}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{S^{\prime}_{1}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\qquad\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}\cap\mathtt{fy}(\alpha)=\emptyset}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}\langle{\underbrace{(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})S_{1}}_{=S}},{}\rangle\xrightarrow{e^{\prime}\vdash\alpha}\langle{\underbrace{(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}@}{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})S^{\prime}_{1}}_{=S^{\prime}}},{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\textstyle\rule[-5.59721pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta^{\prime}}\qquad\Delta=\Delta^{\prime}|_{-\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}}{\textstyle\rule[-2.15277pt]{0.0pt}{12.91663pt}{e}\ \text{\textvisiblespace}\ {\Gamma}\ \vdash\ {(\nu\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}@{\color[rgb]{0.7813,0.3828,0.2031}u})S}\ \triangleright\ {\Delta}}\end{array}$$where on the right we have the typing of $S$ obtained by applying
rule \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[VNew]}$}.
The thesis immediately follows from the inductive hypothesis since
$\mathtt{fy}(S)=\mathtt{fy}(S_{1})\setminus\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}$ and
$\mathtt{fy}(S^{\prime})=\mathtt{fy}(S^{\prime}_{1})\setminus\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}$.
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@{\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[SStr]}$}}
The thesis is immediate by using the inductive hypothesis.
\end{itemize}\end{proof}\par\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{WSI by typing}\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Correspondence between semantics of specifications}Thenextresultsshowthatthedenotationalsemanticsofspecificationscoincideswiththeoperationalrulesgivenin\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:LTStypes}.\par\par\begin{lem}If $\Delta\xrightarrow{\alpha}\Delta^{\prime}$ then

$\Game\big({\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}}\big)=\Game\big({\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}}\big)$ for all
${\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}\in\mathrm{dom}{(\Delta^{\prime})}$. Then, $\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)=\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta^{\prime})$.
\end{lem}\par\begin{proof}It follows by straightforward induction on the derivation of
$r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)$ after noticing that guards are
irrelevant for deriving runs.
\end{proof}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Specifications cover Global types}\par\begin{lem}Let ${}({})\,\triangleq\,{}$ be a global type with
$\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{G})=\{{\mathsf{{p_{0}}}},\ldots,{\mathsf{{p_{n}}}}\}$
and
$\Delta={\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{0}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:\mathsf{G}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{0}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2}(}\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2},}{{\mathsf{{p_{n}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2,0,0.2})}:\mathsf{G}\!\!\upharpoonright\!{{\mathsf{{p}}}_{n}},\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}:{}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}[}{\color[rgb]{0.3,0.3,0.3}]}$.
If $\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})\ \subseteq\ \mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)$, then
$r\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})\subseteq\ \mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta^{\star})$.
\end{lem}\par\begin{proof}By induction on the structure of the derivation
$r\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})$
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RG^{*}_{1}]}$}: then $r\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})$. By hypothesis, $r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)$. By \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTIt1]}$}, $r\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta^{\star})$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RG^{*}_{1}]}$}: then $r=r_{1}[r_{2}]$, $r_{1}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})$ and
$r_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\mathsf{G}^{*^{f}})$. By hypothesis,
$r_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)$.
By inductive hypothesis $r_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta^{\star})$.
By \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTIt2]}$}, $r_{1}[r_{2}]\in\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta^{\star})$.
\end{itemize}\end{proof}\par See\ref{restatable:coveragethm}\begin{proof}We show a stronger result proving that $\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)$, which obviously implies
$\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})\ \Subset\ \mathcal{R}_{\vec{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0.125,0.1719,0.75}y}}}}(\Delta)$.
We proceed by induction on the derivation of $r\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})$. We
proceed by case analysis on the last applied rule in the derivation
of $r\in\mathcal{R}(\mathsf{G})$.
\par\begin{itemize}\par\itemize@item@Case \mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RGEnd]}$}: Follows straightforwardly from rule
\mbox{\footnotesize$\mathsf{[RTEnd]}$} observing that $\mathsf{G}=\mathsf{end}$ and $\Delta$ is
$\mathsf{end}$-only by the definition of projection (\emph{cf.}\ \ on
